=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an undirected tree rooted at node 0 with n nodes numbered from 0 to n - 1, represented by a 2D array edges of length n - 1, where edges[i] = [u_i, v_i, length_i] indicates an edge between nodes u_i and v_i with length length_i. You are also given an integer array nums, where nums[i] represents the value at node i.
A special path is defined as a downward path from an ancestor node to a descendant node such that all the values of the nodes in that path are unique.
Note that a path may start and end at the same node.
Return an array result of size 2, where result[0] is the length of the longest special path, and result[1] is the minimum number of nodes in all possible longest special paths.
 
Example 1:

Input: edges = [[0,1,2],[1,2,3],[1,3,5],[1,4,4],[2,5,6]], nums = [2,1,2,1,3,1]
Output: [6,2]
Explanation:
In the image below, nodes are colored by their corresponding values in nums

The longest special paths are 2 -> 5 and 0 -> 1 -> 4, both having a length of 6. The minimum number of nodes across all longest special paths is 2.

Example 2:

Input: edges = [[1,0,8]], nums = [2,2]
Output: [0,1]
Explanation:

The longest special paths are 0 and 1, both having a length of 0. The minimum number of nodes across all longest special paths is 1.

 
Constraints:

2 <= n <= 5 * 10^4
edges.length == n - 1
edges[i].length == 3
0 <= u_i, v_i < n
1 <= length_i <= 10^3
nums.length == n
0 <= nums[i] <= 5 * 10^4
The input is generated such that edges represents a valid tree.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def longestSpecialPath(self, edges: List[List[int]], nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Alternative recursive:
def dfs(u,parent,d,dist):
    val=nums[u]
    old_last=last[val]; old_second=second[val]
    if old_last != -1:
        second[val]=old_last
        # max_second update
    last[val]=d
    path_dist.append(dist)
    start=max_second+1
    length=dist - path_dist[start]
    nodes=d - start + 1
    update
    for v,w in adj[u]:
       if v!=parent: dfs(v,u,d+1,dist+w)
    # restore
    last[val]=old_last; second[val]=old_second; max_second=prev_max
    path_dist.pop()

Let's design iterative DFS with enter/exit events.

Option 1: Use stack of tuples (u, parent, dist, state) where state 0 enter, 1 exit. On enter:
- d = len(path_dist) (depth)
- append dist to path_dist
- process value update, compute candidate.
- push exit event
- push children enter events? Need process children in any order. But if we push exit first then children, stack LIFO: push exit, then push children; children processed before exit. Need avoid parent. We can push (child,u,dist+w,0) for each neighbor != parent.
But need know depth d for exit? On exit, we can pop path_dist and restore. We don't need d maybe. But need restore value state and max_second. We can store prev values in local variables? If using event stack, on exit we need know old_last, old_second, prev_max, val. We can store these in the exit event tuple, or recompute? Could store on stack: (u, parent, dist, state, old_last, old_second, prev_max, val) maybe. But for enter event, we don't know old values until processing. We can push exit event after processing with captured old values. That is fine.

Pseudo:
adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
for u,v,w in edges: adj[u].append((v,w)); adj[v].append((u,w))
max_val = max(nums) if nums else 0
last = [-1]*(max_val+1)
second = [-1]*(max_val+1)
path_dist = []
max_second = -1
ans_len = 0
ans_nodes = 1 # or inf

stack = [(0, -1, 0, 0)] # state 0 enter? Need include dist from root for node 0 =0.
while stack:
    u, parent, dist, state = stack.pop()
    if state == 0:
        d = len(path_dist)
        path_dist.append(dist)
        val = nums[u]
        old_last = last[val]
        old_second = second[val]
        prev_max = max_second
        if old_last != -1:
            second[val] = old_last
            if old_last > max_second:
                max_second = old_last
        last[val] = d
        # compute candidate
        start = max_second + 1
        length = dist - path_dist[start]
        nodes = d - start + 1
        if length > ans_len:
            ans_len = length; ans_nodes = nodes
        elif length == ans_len:
            if nodes < ans_nodes: ans_nodes = nodes
        # push exit event
        stack.append((u, parent, dist, 1, old_last, old_second, prev_max, val))
        # push children enter events
        for v,w in adj[u]:
            if v != parent:
                stack.append((v, u, dist+w, 0))
    else:
        # exit event tuple has extra fields; but state tuple length differs. Need handle.
        u, parent, dist, state, old_last, old_second, prev_max, val = stack.pop()
        # restore
        last[val] = old_last
        second[val] = old_second
        max_second = prev_max
        path_dist.pop()

Let's write code carefully.

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestSpecialPath(self, edges: List[List[int]], nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(nums)
        adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for u,v,w in edges:
            adj[u].append((v,w))
            adj[v].append((u,w))
        max_val = max(nums) if nums else 0
        last = [-1]*(max_val+1)
        second = [-1]*(max_val+1)
        path_dist = []
        max_second = -1
        ans_len = 0
        ans_nodes = 1  # but if n? We'll update.
        stack = [(0, -1, 0, 0)]  # u, parent, dist, state
        while stack:
            item = stack.pop()
            if item[3] == 0:
                u, parent, dist, state = item
                d = len(path_dist)
                path_dist.append(dist)
                val = nums[u]
                old_last = last[val]
                old_second = second[val]
                prev_max = max_second
                if old_last != -1:
                    second[val] = old_last
                    if old_last > max_second:
                        max_second = old_last
                last[val] = d
                start = max_second + 1
                length = dist - path_dist[start]
                nodes = d - start + 1
                if length > ans_len:
                    ans_len = length
                    ans_nodes = nodes
                elif length == ans_len:
                    if nodes < ans_nodes:
                        ans_nodes = nodes
                stack.append((u, parent, dist, 1, old_last, old_second, prev_max, val))
                for v,w in adj[u]:
                    if v != parent:
                        stack.append((v, u, dist+w, 0))
            else:
                u, parent, dist, state, old_last, old_second, prev_max, val = item
                last[val] = old_last
                second[val] = old_second
                max_second = prev_max
                path_dist.pop()
        return [ans_len, ans_nodes]
```
